H-D

Actor Traceable Actions

We show that input actions, output actions and communication actions are deterministic,

Proposition

VI.3
.

Proposition

VI.3
, restated below, can be decomposed into three parts; namely, input determinacy, output determinacy and communication determinacy.

See
VI.3

Proof.

Follows from
Propositions

G.1
,
G.2
and
G.3
, proven in the dedicated sections below.
∎

H-E

Proving Input Determinacy.

We show that input actions are deterministic,
Proposition

G.1
.
Its proof relies on
Lemma

H.10
below.

Lemma H.10
.

For any
A\equiv A^{\prime}
,
if
K\;|\;O\triangleright A\xrightarrow{\;i?v\;}B
and
K\;|\;O\triangleright A^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\;i?v\;}B^{\prime}
then
B\equiv B^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Suppose
A\equiv A^{\prime}
and

K\;|\;O\triangleright A\xrightarrow{\;i?v\;}B
and

K\;|\;O\triangleright A^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\;i?v\;}B^{\prime}
.
We show
B\equiv B^{\prime}
.
The proof proceeds by induction on the first move.

•

Case
rcv
,
i.e.,

K\;|\;O\triangleright i\mathsf{[}e\,\triangleleft\,q\mathsf{]}\xrightarrow{\;i?v\;}i\mathsf{[}e\,\triangleleft\,q\mathop{:}v\mathsf{]}
.
For the second move, we thus have
K\;|\;O\triangleright A^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\;i?v\;}B^{\prime}

where
i\mathsf{[}e\,\triangleleft\,q\mathsf{]}\equiv A^{\prime}
.
By
Corollary

6
, we obtain
B^{\prime}\equiv i\mathsf{[}e\,\triangleleft\,q\mathop{:}v\mathsf{]}
.
Our result,
i\mathsf{[}e\,\triangleleft\,q\mathop{:}v\mathsf{]}\equiv B^{\prime}
, follows by symmetry.

•

Case
scp1
,
i.e.,

K\;|\;O\triangleright(\nu\,j)A\xrightarrow{\;i?v\;}(\nu\,j)B

because
K,j\;|\;O\triangleright A\xrightarrow{\;i?v\;}B
because
j\,\sharp\,\mathop{\textsl{fn}}(i\textsf{!}v)
.
For the second move, we have
K\;|\;O\triangleright A^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\;i?v\;}B^{\prime}
where
A^{\prime}\equiv(\nu\,j)A
.
By
Lemma

H.8
, we know
K,j\;|\;O\triangleright A\xrightarrow{\;i?v\;}B^{\prime\prime}
and
B^{\prime}\equiv(\nu\,j)B^{\prime\prime}
.
By the IH and the fact that
A\equiv A
, we obtain
B\equiv B^{\prime\prime}
.
Our result,
(\nu\,j)B\equiv(\nu\,j)B^{\prime\prime}
, follows by rule
sCtxS
.

•

Case
parL
,
i.e.,

K\;|\;O\triangleright A_{1}\parallel A_{2}\xrightarrow{\;i?v\;}B_{1}\parallel A_{2}

because
K\;|\;O\triangleright A_{1}\xrightarrow{\;i?v\;}B_{1}
and
\mathop{\textsl{sbj}}(i?v)\,\sharp\,\mathop{\textsl{fId}}(A_{2})
.
For the second move, we have
K\;|\;O\triangleright A^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\;i?v\;}B^{\prime}
where
A^{\prime}\equiv A_{1}\parallel A_{2}
.
By
Lemma

H.6
,
Corollary

5
and
i\,\sharp\,\mathop{\textsl{fId}}(A_{2})
, we know
K\;|\;O\triangleright A_{1}\xrightarrow{\;i?v\;}B_{1}^{\prime}
and
B^{\prime}\equiv B_{1}^{\prime}\parallel A_{2}
for some
B_{1}^{\prime}
.
By the IH,

B_{1}\equiv B_{1}^{\prime}
.
Our result,
B_{1}\parallel A_{2}\equiv B_{1}^{\prime}\parallel A_{2}
, follows by
sCtxP
.

•

Case
parR
, proof is analogous to that for case
parL
.

•

Case
str
,
i.e.,

K\;|\;O\triangleright A\xrightarrow{\;i?v\;}B

because
A\equiv A^{\prime\prime}
and
K\;|\;O\triangleright A^{\prime\prime}\xrightarrow{\;i?v\;}B^{\prime\prime}
and
B^{\prime\prime}\equiv B
.
For the second move, we have
K\;|\;O\triangleright A^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\;i?v\;}B^{\prime}
where
A^{\prime}\equiv A^{\prime\prime}
.
By transitivity, we know
A^{\prime\prime}\equiv A^{\prime}
as well.
Using the IH, we thus obtain that
B^{\prime\prime}\equiv B^{\prime}
.
Our result,
B\equiv B^{\prime}
, follows by symmetry and transitivity. ∎

See
G.1

Proof.

Follows from
Lemma

H.10
since
A\equiv A
.
∎

H-F

Proving Output Determinacy

The proof showing that output actions are deterministic,
Proposition

G.2
, relies on
Lemmas

H.11
and
H.12
.
We start with the former, which describes the structure of actors capable of performing an output action
i\textsf{!}v
.

Lemma H.11
.

If
K\;|\;O\triangleright A\xrightarrow{\;i\textsf{!}v\;}B

then
A\equiv A^{\prime}\parallel i\langle v\rangle
and
B\equiv A^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Suppose
K\;|\;O\triangleright A\xrightarrow{\;i\textsf{!}v\;}B
.
The proof proceeds by induction on
K\;|\;O\triangleright A\xrightarrow{\;i\textsf{!}v\;}B
.

•

Case
snd2
,
i.e.,

K\;|\;O\triangleright i\langle v\rangle\xrightarrow{\;i\textsf{!}v\;}\mathbf{0}
where
i\in O
.
Result is immediate by rules
sNil
,
sCom
.

•

Case
scp1
,
i.e.,

K\;|\;O\triangleright(\nu\,j)A\xrightarrow{\;i\textsf{!}v\;}(\nu\,j)B

because
K,j\;|\;O\triangleright A\xrightarrow{\;i\textsf{!}v\;}B
and
j\,\sharp\,\mathop{\textsl{fn}}(i\textsf{!}v)
.
By the IH, we obtain that

A\equiv A^{\prime}\parallel i\langle v\rangle
and

B\equiv A^{\prime}
for some
A^{\prime}
.
Applying rule
sCtxt
, we get
(\nu\,j)A\equiv(\nu\,j)(A^{\prime}\parallel i\langle v\rangle)
and
(\nu\,j)B\equiv(\nu\,j)A^{\prime}
.

There only remains to show that
(\nu\,j)A\equiv((\nu\,j)A^{\prime})\parallel i\langle v\rangle
.
Since
j\,\sharp\,\mathop{\textsl{fn}}(i\textsf{!}v)
, we know
j\,\sharp\,\mathop{\textsl{fn}}(i\langle v\rangle)
.
Thus, by rule
sExt
,
(\nu\,j)(A^{\prime}\parallel i\langle v\rangle)\equiv((\nu\,j)A^{\prime})\parallel i\langle v\rangle
.
Our result,
(\nu\,j)A\equiv((\nu\,j)A^{\prime})\parallel i\langle v\rangle
, follows by transitivity.

•

Case
parL
,
i.e.,

K\;|\;O\triangleright A_{1}\parallel A_{2}\xrightarrow{\;i\textsf{!}v\;}B_{1}\parallel A_{2}

because
K\;|\;O\triangleright A_{1}\xrightarrow{\;i\textsf{!}v\;}B_{1}
and
i\,\sharp\,\mathop{\textsl{fId}}(A_{2})
.
By the IH, we obtain
A_{1}\equiv A_{1}^{\prime}\parallel i\langle v\rangle

and
B_{1}\equiv A_{1}^{\prime}
for some
A_{1}^{\prime}
.
Applying rule
sCtxP
, we get

A_{1}\parallel A_{2}\equiv(A_{1}^{\prime}\parallel i\langle v\rangle)\parallel A_{2}
and

B_{1}\parallel A_{2}\equiv A_{1}^{\prime}\parallel A_{2}
.

There remains to show
A_{1}\parallel A_{2}\equiv(A_{1}^{\prime}\parallel A_{2})\parallel i\langle v\rangle
; this follows by rules
sAss
,
sCom
.

•

Case
parR
, analogous to that of
parL
.

•

Case
sTrn
,
i.e.,

K\;|\;O\triangleright A\xrightarrow{\;i\textsf{!}v\;}B

because
A\equiv A^{\prime\prime}
,
B\equiv B^{\prime}
and
K\;|\;O\triangleright A^{\prime\prime}\xrightarrow{\;i\langle v\rangle\;}B^{\prime}
.
By the IH,
A^{\prime\prime}\equiv A^{\prime}\parallel i\langle v\rangle
and
B^{\prime}\equiv A^{\prime}
.
By transitivity and symmetry of
\equiv
, we can thus conclude
A\equiv A^{\prime}\parallel i\langle v\rangle
and
B\equiv A^{\prime}
.
∎

Lemma H.12
.

For any
A\equiv A^{\prime}
,
if
K\;|\;O\triangleright A\xrightarrow{\;i\textsf{!}v\;}B
and
K\;|\;O\triangleright A^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\;i\textsf{!}v\;}B^{\prime}
then
B\equiv B^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Suppose
A\equiv A^{\prime}
and
K\;|\;O\triangleright A\xrightarrow{\;i\textsf{!}v\;}B
and
K\;|\;O\triangleright A^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\;i\textsf{!}v\;}B^{\prime}
.
We show
B\equiv B^{\prime}
.
The proof proceeds by induction on the first move.

•

Case
snd2
,
i.e.,

K\;|\;O\triangleright i\langle v\rangle\xrightarrow{\;i\textsf{!}v\;}\mathbf{0}
where
i\in O
.
For the second move, we have
K\;|\;O\triangleright A^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\;i\textsf{!}v\;}B^{\prime}
where
A^{\prime}\equiv i\langle v\rangle
.
By
Lemma

H.11
, we know
A^{\prime}\equiv A^{\prime\prime}\parallel i\langle v\rangle
and
B^{\prime}\equiv A^{\prime\prime}
for some
A^{\prime\prime}
.
But since
A^{\prime}\equiv i\langle v\rangle
, we can show
A^{\prime\prime}\equiv\mathbf{0}
.
Our result,
\mathbf{0}\equiv B^{\prime}
, follows.

•

Case
scp1
,
i.e.,

K\;|\;O\triangleright(\nu\,j)A\xrightarrow{\;i\textsf{!}v\;}(\nu\,j)B

because
K,j\;|\;O\triangleright A\xrightarrow{\;i\textsf{!}v\;}B
and
j\,\sharp\,\mathop{\textsl{fn}}(v)\cup\{i\}
.
Consider the second move,
K\;|\;O\triangleright A^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\;i\textsf{!}v\;}B^{\prime}
where
(\nu\,j)A\equiv A^{\prime}
.
By
Lemma

H.8
, we know

B^{\prime}\equiv(\nu\,j)B^{\prime\prime}

and
K,j\;|\;O\triangleright A\xrightarrow{\;i\textsf{!}v\;}B^{\prime\prime}
.
By the IH, we obtain that
B\equiv B^{\prime\prime}
.
By rule
sCtxS
,
(\nu\,j)B\equiv(\nu\,j)B^{\prime\prime}
.
Our result,
(\nu\,j)B\equiv B^{\prime}
, follows by transitivity/symmetry.

•

Case
parL
,
i.e.,

K\;|\;O\triangleright A_{1}\parallel A_{2}\xrightarrow{\;i\textsf{!}v\;}B_{1}\parallel A_{2}

because
K\;|\;O\triangleright A_{1}\xrightarrow{\;i\textsf{!}v\;}B_{1}
and
i\,\sharp\,\mathop{\textsl{fId}}(A_{2})
.
Consider the second move,
K\;|\;O\triangleright A^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\;i\textsf{!}v\;}B^{\prime}
where
A_{1}\parallel A_{2}\equiv A^{\prime}
.
By
Lemma

H.7
, we have two sub-cases:

–

For the first subcase,
K\;|\;O\triangleright A_{1}\xrightarrow{\;i\textsf{!}v\;}B_{1}^{\prime}
and

B^{\prime}\equiv B_{1}^{\prime}\parallel A_{2}
.
By the IH, we obtain
B_{1}\equiv B_{1}^{\prime}
.
By rule
sCxtP
,
B_{1}\parallel A_{2}\equiv B_{1}^{\prime}\parallel A_{2}
.
Our result,
B_{1}\parallel A_{2}\equiv B^{\prime}
, follows by transitivity/symmetry.

–

For the second subcase,
K\;|\;O\triangleright A_{2}\xrightarrow{\;i\textsf{!}v\;}B_{2}
and

B^{\prime}\equiv A_{1}\parallel B_{2}
.

Lemma

H.11
, we obtain

A_{1}\equiv A_{1}^{\prime}\parallel i\langle v\rangle
and

A_{2}\equiv A_{2}^{\prime}\parallel i\langle v\rangle
and

B_{1}\equiv A_{1}^{\prime}
and
B_{2}\equiv A_{2}^{\prime}
.
This implies that

\displaystyle B_{1}\parallel A_{2}

\displaystyle\equiv A_{1}^{\prime}\parallel(A_{2}^{\prime}\parallel i\langle v\rangle)

\displaystyle\equiv(A_{1}^{\prime}\parallel i\langle v\rangle)\parallel A_{2}^{\prime}\text{ using rules }\textsc{sCom}\text{ and }\textsc{sAss}

\displaystyle\equiv A_{1}\parallel B_{2}\equiv B^{\prime}

Our result,
B_{1}\parallel A_{2}\equiv B^{\prime}
, follows by transitivity.

•

Case
parR
, analogous to that of
parL
.

•

Case
str
,
i.e.,

K\;|\;O\triangleright A\xrightarrow{\;i\textsf{!}v\;}B

because
A\equiv A^{\prime\prime}
and
K\;|\;O\triangleright A^{\prime\prime}\xrightarrow{\;i\textsf{!}v\;}B^{\prime\prime}
and
B^{\prime\prime}\equiv B
.
For the second move, we have
K\;|\;O\triangleright A^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\;i\textsf{!}v\;}B^{\prime}
where
A^{\prime}\equiv A^{\prime\prime}
.
By transitivity, we know
A^{\prime\prime}\equiv A^{\prime}
as well.
Using the IH, we thus obtain that
B^{\prime\prime}\equiv B^{\prime}
.
Our result,
B\equiv B^{\prime}
, follows by symmetry and transitivity. ∎

See
G.2

Proof.

Follows from
Lemma

H.12
since
A\equiv A
.
∎

H-G

Proving Communication Determinacy

The proof for
Proposition

G.3
relies on
Lemma

H.13
below, which describes the structure of an actor system capable of performing an internal communication action
\mathsf{com}(i,v)
.

Lemma H.13
.

If
K\;|\;O\triangleright A\xrightarrow{\;\mathsf{com}(i,v)\;}B
then

(i)

A\equiv A^{\prime}\parallel i\langle v\rangle
;

(ii)

K\;|\;O\triangleright A^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\;i?v\;}B^{\prime}
for some
B^{\prime}
;

(iii)

B\equiv B^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Assume
K\;|\;O\triangleright A\xrightarrow{\;\mathsf{com}(i,v)\;}B
.
We proceed by rule induction, outlining only the main cases; the remaining follow similarly.

•

Case
commL
,
i.e.,

A=A_{1}\parallel A_{2}
and
B=B_{1}\parallel B_{2}

because
K\;|\;\mathop{\textsl{fId}}(A_{2})\triangleright A_{1}\xrightarrow{\;i\textsf{!}v\;}B_{1}

and
K\;|\;\mathop{\textsl{fId}}(A_{1})\triangleright A_{2}\xrightarrow{\;i?v\;}B_{2}
.

By
Lemma

H.11
, we know
A_{1}\equiv A_{1}^{\prime}\parallel i\langle v\rangle

and
B_{1}\equiv A_{1}^{\prime}
.
By rules
sCtxP, sAss, sCom
and transitivity, this implies

A_{1}\parallel A_{2}\equiv(A_{1}^{\prime}\parallel A_{2})\parallel i\langle v\rangle
, giving us
(i)
.

Applying rule
parR
on
K\;|\;\mathop{\textsl{fId}}(A_{1})\triangleright A_{2}\xrightarrow{\;i?v\;}B_{2}
, we obtain

K\;|\;\mathop{\textsl{fId}}(A_{1})\triangleright A_{1}^{\prime}\parallel A_{2}\xrightarrow{\;i?v\;}A_{1}^{\prime}\parallel B_{2}
.
Using
Lemma

H.3
, we get
K\;|\;O\triangleright A_{1}^{\prime}\parallel A_{2}\xrightarrow{\;i?v\;}A_{1}^{\prime}\parallel B_{2}
,
giving us
(ii)
.

The result in
(iii)
, namely
B_{1}\parallel B_{2}\equiv A_{1}^{\prime}\parallel B_{2}
, follows from
B_{1}\equiv A_{1}^{\prime}
and rule
sCtxP
.

•

Case
parL
,
i.e.,

A=A_{1}\parallel A_{2}
and
B=B_{1}\parallel A_{2}
because

K\;|\;O\triangleright A_{1}\xrightarrow{\;\mathsf{com}(i,v)\;}B_{1}
.
By the IH, we obtain that

\displaystyle A_{1}\equiv A_{1}^{\prime}\parallel i\langle v\rangle

(13)

\displaystyle K\;|\;O\triangleright A_{1}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\;i?v\;}B_{1}^{\prime}

(14)

\displaystyle B_{1}\equiv B_{1}^{\prime}

(15)

The result in
(i)
, namely
A_{1}\parallel A_{2}\equiv(A_{1}^{\prime}\parallel A_{2})\parallel i\langle v\rangle
, follows by (
13
), rules
sCtxP
,
sAss
,
sCom
and transitivity.

The result in
(ii)
, namely
K\;|\;O\triangleright A_{1}^{\prime}\parallel A_{2}\xrightarrow{\;i?v\;}B_{1}^{\prime}\parallel A_{2}
, follows by (
14
) and rule
parL
.

The result in
(iii)
, namely
B_{1}\parallel A_{2}\equiv B_{1}^{\prime}\parallel A_{2}
, follows from (
15
) and rule
sCtxP
.

•

Case
scp1
,

A=(\nu\,j)A^{\prime}
and
B=(\nu\,i)B^{\prime}

because
K,j\triangleright A^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\;\mathsf{com}(i,v)\;}B^{\prime}
and
j\,\sharp\,\mathop{\textsl{fn}}(\mathsf{com}(i,v))

where
\mathop{\textsl{fn}}(\mathsf{com}(i,v))=\{i,v\}
.
By the IH, we obtain

\displaystyle A^{\prime}\equiv B^{\prime\prime}\parallel i\langle v\rangle

(16)

\displaystyle K,j\;|\;O\triangleright A^{\prime\prime}\xrightarrow{\;i?v\;}B^{\prime\prime}

(17)

\displaystyle B^{\prime}\equiv B^{\prime\prime}

(18)

Applying rule
sCtxS
on (
16
), we get
(\nu\,j)A^{\prime}\equiv(\nu\,j)(A^{\prime\prime}\parallel i\langle v\rangle)
.
Since
j\,\sharp\,\{i,v\}
, we can use rule
sExt
to obtain
(\nu\,j)A^{\prime}\equiv((\nu\,j)A^{\prime\prime})\parallel i\langle v\rangle
, giving us the result in
(i)
.

The result in
(ii)
, namely
K\;|\;O\triangleright(\nu\,j)A^{\prime\prime}\xrightarrow{\;i?v\;}(\nu\,j)B^{\prime\prime}
, follows by (
17
) and rule
scp1
.

The result in
(iii)
, namely
(\nu\,j)B^{\prime}\equiv(\nu\,j)B^{\prime\prime}
, follows by (
18
) and rule
sCtxS
.
∎

We are now in a position to prove that communication actions lead to structurally equivalent actor systems, as stated in
Proposition

G.3
below.

See
G.3

Proof.

Suppose
K\;|\;O\triangleright A\xrightarrow{\;\mathsf{com}(i,v)\;}B
and
K\;|\;O\triangleright A\xrightarrow{\;\mathsf{com}(i,v)\;}B^{\prime}
.
We need to show
B\equiv B^{\prime}
.
By
Lemma

H.13
and
K\;|\;O\triangleright A\xrightarrow{\;\mathsf{com}(i,v)\;}B
, we know that there exist some actor system
C
such that

\displaystyle A\equiv C\parallel i\langle v\rangle\quad\text{and}\quad K\;|\;O\triangleright C\xrightarrow{\;i?v\;}C^{\prime}\quad\text{and}\quad B\equiv C^{\prime}

Similarly, by
Lemma

H.13
and
K\;|\;O\triangleright A\xrightarrow{\;\mathsf{com}(i,v)\;}B^{\prime}
, we know that there exist some actor system
D
such that

\displaystyle A\equiv D\parallel i\langle v\rangle\quad\text{and}\quad K\;|\;O\triangleright D\xrightarrow{\;i?v\;}D^{\prime}\quad\text{and}\quad B^{\prime}\equiv D^{\prime}

Since
A\equiv C\parallel i\langle v\rangle
and
A\equiv D\parallel i\langle v\rangle
,
we also know that
C\parallel i\langle v\rangle\equiv D\parallel i\langle v\rangle
.
By case analysis, this could have only been derived using rule
sCtxP
, which gives us
C\equiv D
.
Thus, by rule
sTrn
and
K\;|\;O\triangleright D\xrightarrow{\;i?v\;}D^{\prime}
, we know
K\;|\;O\triangleright C\xrightarrow{\;i?v\;}D^{\prime}
as well.
Using the facts that
K\;|\;O\triangleright C\xrightarrow{\;i?v\;}D^{\prime}
and
K\;|\;O\triangleright C\xrightarrow{\;i?v\;}C^{\prime}
and
Proposition

G.1
, we obtain
C^{\prime}\equiv D^{\prime}
.
Since
B\equiv C^{\prime}
and
B^{\prime}\equiv D^{\prime}
, using transitivity/symmetry, we can conclude
B\equiv B^{\prime}
.
∎

Appendix I
Properties of the Violation Relation

We prove some properties about the violation relation
\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}
of
Definition

VII.3
, including
Theorems

VII.1
and
VII.2
from
Section

V
.
In this section and the ones that follow, we work up to
\alpha
-equivalence.

Lemma I.1
.

If
(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi
then
H\neq\emptyset
.

Proof.

Straightforward by rule induction.
∎

We show that the violation relation
\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}
observes sanity checks akin to those for the history analysis of
Figure

3
.
In particular,
Propositions

I.3
and
I.2
below guarantee that once a system violates a formula via a history, it will persistently violate that formula, regardless of any other behaviour it might exhibit (described in terms of additional traces added to the history, width, or longer trace prefixes, length).

Proposition I.2

(Width Irrevocability)
.

If
(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi
then
(H\cup H^{\prime},\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to that for
Proposition

IV.3
.
∎

Proposition I.3

(Length Irrevocability)
.

If
(H\cup{t},\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi
then
(H\cup\{tu\},\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to that for
Proposition

IV.3
.
∎

Corollary 7
.

If
(H\cup H^{\prime},\textsf{f\,})\not{\models_{Det}}\varphi
then
(H,\textsf{f\,})\not{\models_{Det}}\varphi
.

We also lift the function
\mathop{\textsl{sub}}(-)
to traces:

\mathop{\textsl{sub}}(H,\epsilon)=H
and
\mathop{\textsl{sub}}(H,\mu t)=\mathop{\textsl{sub}}(\mathop{\textsl{sub}}(H,\mu),t)
.
Similarly,
\textsc{Det}(\epsilon)=\mathsf{true}
and
\textsc{Det}(\mu t)=\textsc{Det}(\mu)\wedge\textsc{Det}(t)
.

Lemma I.4
.

For all
\varphi\in\textsc{sHML}^{\vee}_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}

and
t\in\textsc{iAct}^{*}
,
if
H^{\prime}\!=\!\mathop{\textsl{sub}}(H,t\alpha)
and
\textsf{f\,}^{\prime}\!=\!\textsf{f\,}\wedge\textsc{Det}(t\alpha)
and
(H^{\prime},\textsf{f\,}^{\prime})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi

then
(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}[\alpha]\varphi
.

Proof.

The proof proceeds by induction on the length of
t
,
i.e.,

n=|t|
.

•

When
n=0
, then
t=\epsilon
,
H^{\prime}=\mathop{\textsl{sub}}(H,\alpha)
,
\textsf{f\,}^{\prime}=\textsf{f\,}\wedge\textsc{Det}(\alpha)
and
(H^{\prime},\textsf{f\,}^{\prime})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi
.
Our result,
(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}[\alpha]\varphi
, follows immediately by rule
vUm
.

•

When
n=k+1
,
then
t=\gamma_{1}\cdots\gamma_{n}\in\textsc{iAct}^{*}

and
H^{\prime}=\mathop{\textsl{sub}}(H,t\alpha)

and
\textsf{f\,}^{\prime}=\textsf{f\,}\wedge\textsc{Det}(t\alpha)

and
(H^{\prime},\textsf{f\,}^{\prime})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi
.
By definition of
\mathop{\textsl{sub}}(-)
, we know there exists some
H^{\prime\prime}
such that

\displaystyle H^{\prime\prime}=\mathop{\textsl{sub}}(H,\gamma_{1})\quad\text{ and }\quad H^{\prime}=\mathop{\textsl{sub}}(H^{\prime},\gamma_{2}\cdots\gamma_{n}\alpha)

(19)

By definition of
\textsc{Det}(-)
, we also know there exists some
\textsf{f\,}^{\prime\prime}
such that

\displaystyle\textsf{f\,}^{\prime\prime}=\textsf{f\,}\wedge\textsc{Det}(\gamma_{1})\quad\text{ and }\quad\textsf{f\,}^{\prime}=\textsf{f\,}^{\prime\prime}\wedge\textsc{Det}(\gamma_{2}\cdots\gamma_{n})

(20)

Using (
19
), (
20
) and the IH, we obtain
(H^{\prime\prime},\textsf{f\,}^{\prime\prime})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}[\alpha]\varphi
.
Our result,
(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}[\alpha]\varphi
, follows by applying rule
vUmPre
.
∎

We prove that whenever a system
p
produces a history
H
that violates a formula
\varphi
,
i.e.,

H\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi
, then
p
must also violate it,
i.e.,

p\notin\llbracket\varphi\rrbracket
, namely
Theorem

VII.1
from
Section

V
.
This proof relies on an additional result.
Specifically,
Lemma

I.5
below states that if a history violates a formula with the flag set to
\mathsf{false}
, then a single trace
t
suffices to violate that formula.

Lemma I.5
.

If
(H,\mathsf{false})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi
then
\exists t\in H
such that
(\{t\},\mathsf{false})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi
.

Proof.

Straightforward by rule induction.
∎

Lemma

I.6
below then states that whenever a single trace violates a formula, then the system producing that trace also violates the formula.

Lemma I.6
.

For all
t\in T_{p}
,
if
(\{t\},\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi
then
p\notin\llbracket\varphi\rrbracket
.

Proof.

Straightforward by rule induction.
∎

We are now in a position to prove
Theorem

VII.1
, restated below.

See
VII.1

Proof.

Suppose that
\exists H\subseteq T_{p}
such that
H\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi
.
Our result,
p\notin\llbracket\varphi\rrbracket
, follows from
Lemma

I.7
below, by letting
\textsf{f\,}=\mathsf{true}
.
∎

Lemma I.7
.

For all
\varphi\in\textsc{sHML}^{\vee}_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}
and
H\subseteq T_{p}
,
if
(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi
then
p\notin\llbracket\varphi\rrbracket
.

Proof.

The proof proceeds by induction on
(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi
where
H\subseteq T_{p}
.

•

Case
vF
,
i.e.,

(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\mathsf{ff}
where
H\neq\emptyset
. Our result,
p\notin\llbracket\mathsf{ff}\rrbracket
, is immediate since
\llbracket\mathsf{ff}\rrbracket=\emptyset
.

•

Case
vUm
,
i.e.,

(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}[\alpha]\varphi
because
(H^{\prime},\textsf{f\,}^{\prime})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi

where
H^{\prime}=\mathop{\textsl{sub}}(H,\alpha)
and
\textsf{f\,}^{\prime}=\textsf{f\,}\wedge\textsc{Det}(\alpha)
.
By
Lemma

I.1
, we also know
H^{\prime}\neq\emptyset
.
This means that
\exists n\geq 1
such that

\displaystyle H^{\prime}=\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}H_{i}^{\prime}\text{ such that }p\xRightarrow{\;\alpha\;}_{\!\raisebox{2.2604pt}{\tiny$T$}}q_{i}\text{ and }H_{i}^{\prime}\subseteq T_{q_{i}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{ for each }i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}

(21)

There are two subcases to consider:

–

If
\textsf{f\,}^{\prime}=\mathsf{false}
,
we know by
Lemma

I.5
that
\exists t\in H^{\prime}
such that
(\{t\},\textsf{f\,}^{\prime})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}[\alpha]\varphi
.
From (
21
), we also know
t\in H^{\prime}_{k}
for some
k\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
and that
p\xRightarrow{\;\alpha\;}_{\!\raisebox{2.2604pt}{\tiny$T$}}q_{k}
and
H^{\prime}_{k}\subseteq T_{q_{k}}
.
Using
Lemma

I.6
, we deduce that
q_{k}\notin\llbracket\varphi\rrbracket
, and by
Proposition

A.5
, we obtain
p\xRightarrow{\;\alpha\;}q_{k}
.
Thus, we can conclude that
p\notin\{q\;|\;p\xRightarrow{\;\alpha\;}q\text{ implies }q\notin\llbracket\varphi\rrbracket\}=\llbracket[\alpha]\varphi\rrbracket
, as required.

–

If
\textsf{f\,}^{\prime}=\mathsf{true}
, then
\textsc{Det}(\alpha)
.
From
Lemma

A.4
, we know
T_{q_{i}}=T_{q_{j}}
for all
i,j\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
,
which implies
H^{\prime}\subseteq T_{q_{k}}
for all
k\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
.
We can thus use the IH and obtain
q_{k}\notin\llbracket\varphi\rrbracket
.
By
Proposition

A.5
and the fact that
p\xRightarrow{\;\alpha\;}_{\!\raisebox{2.2604pt}{\tiny$T$}}q_{k}
, we also know
p\xRightarrow{\;\alpha\;}q_{k}
.
Thus, we can conclude that
p\notin\{q\;|\;p\xRightarrow{\;\alpha\;}q\text{ implies }q\notin\llbracket\varphi\rrbracket\}=\llbracket[\alpha]\varphi\rrbracket
, as required.

•

Case
vUmPre
,
i.e.,

(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}[\alpha]\varphi
because
(H^{\prime},\textsf{f\,}^{\prime})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}[\alpha]\varphi

where
H^{\prime}=\mathop{\textsl{sub}}(H,\gamma)
and
\textsf{f\,}^{\prime}=\textsf{f\,}\wedge\textsc{Det}(\gamma)
.
Due to our assumption that all internal actions
iAct
are deterministic,
i.e.,

\textsc{Det}(\gamma)
, then
\textsf{f\,}^{\prime}=\mathsf{true}
.
By
Lemma

I.1
, we also know
H^{\prime}\neq\emptyset
.
This means that
\exists n\geq 1
such that

\displaystyle H^{\prime}=\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}H_{i}^{\prime}\text{ such that }p\xRightarrow{\;\gamma\;}_{\!\raisebox{2.2604pt}{\tiny$T$}}q_{i}\text{ and }H_{i}^{\prime}\subseteq T_{q_{i}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{ for each }i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}

From
Lemma

A.4
, we know
T_{q_{i}}=T_{q_{j}}
for all
i,j\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
,
which implies
H^{\prime}\subseteq T_{q_{k}}
for all
k\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
.
We can thus use the IH and obtain
q_{k}\notin\llbracket[\alpha]\varphi\rrbracket
.
By
Proposition

A.5
and
p\xRightarrow{\;\gamma\;}_{\!\raisebox{2.2604pt}{\tiny$T$}}q_{k}
, we also know
p\xRightarrow{\;\;\;\;}q_{k}
.
Our result,
p\notin\llbracket[\alpha]\varphi\rrbracket
, follows by definition of
\llbracket-\rrbracket
.

•

Case
vAndL
.
We know
(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi\wedge\psi
because
(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi
.
By the IH, we obtain
p\notin\llbracket\varphi\rrbracket
, which implies
that
p\notin\llbracket\varphi\rrbracket\cap\llbracket\psi\rrbracket=\llbracket\varphi\wedge\psi\rrbracket
.

•

Case
vAndR
.
Proof is analogous to that for
vAndL
.

•

Case
vOr
.
We know
(H,\mathsf{true})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi\vee\psi
because
(H,\mathsf{true})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi
and
(H,\mathsf{true})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\psi
.
By the IH, we obtain
p\notin\llbracket\varphi\rrbracket
and
p\notin\llbracket\psi\rrbracket
, which implies
that
p\notin\llbracket\varphi\rrbracket\cup\llbracket\psi\rrbracket=\llbracket\varphi\vee\psi\rrbracket
.

•

Case
vMax
.
We know
(p,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\mathsf{max}\,\mathsf{\mathsf{X}}.\varphi
because
(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi[\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\mathsf{max}\,\mathsf{\mathsf{X}}.\varphi$}\!/\!\mbox{\small$\mathsf{X}$}]
.
By the IH, we obtain
p\notin\llbracket\varphi[\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\mathsf{max}\,\mathsf{\mathsf{X}}.\varphi$}\!/\!\mbox{\small$\mathsf{X}$}]\rrbracket=\llbracket\mathsf{max}\,\mathsf{\mathsf{X}}.\varphi\rrbracket
.
∎

We now prove
Theorem

VII.2
, restated below.

See
VII.2

Proof.

Follows from
Lemma

I.8
below, by letting
\textsf{f\,}=\mathsf{true}
.
∎

Lemma I.8
.

If
\textsc{Det}(\gamma)=\mathsf{true}
for all
\gamma\in\textsc{iAct}
, then
for all
p\in\textsc{Prc}
,
\varphi\in\textsc{sHML}^{\vee}_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}
and
\textsf{f\,}\in\textsc{Bool}
,
if
\textsf{f\,}\vdash_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi
and
p\notin\llbracket\varphi\rrbracket

then
\bigl(\exists H\subseteq T_{p}
such that
(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi\bigr)
.

Proof.

Suppose
p\in\llbracket\varphi\rrbracket
.
Since
H\subseteq T_{p}
, it suffices to show
(T_{p},\mathsf{true})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi
.
This follows from
Lemma

I.9
below.
∎

Lemma I.9
.

If
\textsc{Det}(\gamma)=\mathsf{true}
for all
\gamma\in\textsc{iAct}
, then
for all
p\in\textsc{Prc}
,
\varphi\in\textsc{sHML}^{\vee}_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}
and
\textsf{f\,}\in\textsc{Bool}
,
if
\textsf{f\,}\vdash_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi

and
p\notin\llbracket\varphi\rrbracket
then
(T_{p},\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi
.

Proof.

The proof proceeds by rule induction on
\textsf{f\,}\vdash_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi
.

•

Case
cA
,
i.e.,

\textsf{f\,}\vdash_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi
where
\varphi\in\{\mathsf{ff},\mathsf{tt},\mathsf{X}\}
.
Assume that
p\notin\llbracket\varphi\rrbracket
.
When
\varphi=\mathsf{ff}
, we immediately obtain that
(T_{p},\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\mathsf{ff}
by rule
vF
.
When
\varphi=\mathsf{tt}
, the statement is vacuously true since
\llbracket\mathsf{tt}\rrbracket=\textsc{Prc}
and thus
p\in\llbracket\varphi\rrbracket
.
Also, we cannot have that
\varphi=\mathsf{X}
; we are assuming
\varphi
is closed.

•

Case
cUm
,
i.e.,

\textsf{f\,}\vdash_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}[\alpha]\varphi
because
\textsf{f\,}\wedge\textsc{Det}(\alpha)\vdash_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi
.
Assume
p\notin\llbracket[\alpha]\varphi\rrbracket
.
This means that there exists
q
such that
p\xRightarrow{\;\alpha\;}q
and
q\notin\llbracket\varphi\rrbracket
.
By
Proposition

A.5
, we know
p\xRightarrow{\;t\;}_{\!\raisebox{2.2604pt}{\tiny$T$}}p^{\prime}\xRightarrow{\;\alpha\;}_{\!\raisebox{2.2604pt}{\tiny$T$}}p^{\prime\prime}\xRightarrow{\;t^{\prime}\;}_{\!\raisebox{2.2604pt}{\tiny$T$}}q
for some
p^{\prime},p^{\prime\prime}
and
t,t^{\prime}\in\textsc{iAct}^{*}
,
which implies
p^{\prime\prime}\notin\llbracket\varphi\rrbracket
.
There are three subcases to consider:

–

When
\textsf{f\,}{=}\mathsf{true}{=}\textsc{Det}(\alpha)
, we have
\mathsf{true}\vdash_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi
.
By the IH, we deduce
(T_{p^{\prime\prime}},\mathsf{true})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi
,
i.e.,

(T_{p^{\prime\prime}},\mathsf{true}\wedge\textsc{Det}(\alpha))\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi
.
Applying rule
vUm
, we obtain
(T_{p^{\prime}},\mathsf{true})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}[\alpha]\varphi
.
Applying rule
vUmPre

|t|
times, we conclude
(T_{p},\mathsf{true})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}[\alpha]\varphi
.

–

When
\textsf{f\,}=\mathsf{false}
, the proof is analogous to that for the previous case.

–

When
\textsf{f\,}=\mathsf{true}
and
\textsc{Det}(\alpha)=\mathsf{false}
, we have
\mathsf{false}\vdash_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi
.
By the IH, we get
(T_{p^{\prime\prime}},\mathsf{false})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi

i.e.,

(T_{p^{\prime\prime}},\mathsf{true}\wedge\textsc{Det}(\alpha))\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi
.
Applying rule
vUm
, we obtain
(T_{p^{\prime}},\mathsf{true})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}[\alpha]\varphi
.
By the assumption that
\textsc{Det}(\gamma)=\mathsf{true}
, then we also know that for
t=\gamma_{1}\cdots\gamma_{n}
, we have
\textsc{Det}(\gamma_{i})=\mathsf{true}
.
We can thus apply rule
vUmPre

n
times and conclude
(T_{p},\mathsf{true})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}[\alpha]\varphi
.

•

Case
cAnd
,
i.e.,

\textsf{f\,}\vdash_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi\wedge\psi
because
\textsf{f\,}\vdash_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi
and
\textsf{f\,}\vdash_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\psi
.
Assume
p\notin\llbracket\varphi\wedge\psi\rrbracket
.
This implies that either
p\notin\llbracket\varphi\rrbracket
or
p\notin\llbracket\psi\rrbracket
.

W.l.o.g.
suppose the former.
By the IH, we obtain
(T_{p},\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi
.
Our result,
(T_{p},\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi\wedge\psi
, follows by rule
vAnd
.

•

Case
cOr
,
i.e.,

\mathsf{true}\vdash_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi\vee\psi
because
\mathsf{true}\vdash_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi
and
\mathsf{true}\vdash_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\psi
.
Assume
p\notin\llbracket\varphi\vee\psi\rrbracket
.
This implies that
p\notin\llbracket\varphi\rrbracket
and
p\notin\llbracket\psi\rrbracket
.
By the IH, we obtain
(T_{p},\mathsf{true})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi
and
(T_{p},\mathsf{true})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\psi
.
Our result,
(T_{p},\mathsf{true})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi\vee\psi
, follows by rule
vOr
.

•

Case
cMax
,
i.e.,

\textsf{f\,}\vdash_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\mathsf{max}\,\mathsf{\mathsf{X}}.\varphi
because
\textsf{f\,}\vdash_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi[\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\mathsf{max}\,\mathsf{\mathsf{X}}.\varphi$}\!/\!\mbox{\small$\mathsf{X}$}]
.
Assume
p\notin\llbracket\mathsf{max}\,\mathsf{\mathsf{X}}.\varphi\rrbracket
.
Since
\llbracket\mathsf{max}\,\mathsf{\mathsf{X}}.\varphi\rrbracket=\llbracket\varphi[\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\mathsf{max}\,\mathsf{\mathsf{X}}.\varphi$}\!/\!\mbox{\small$\mathsf{X}$}]\rrbracket
, we also know
p\in\llbracket\varphi[\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\mathsf{max}\,\mathsf{\mathsf{X}}.\varphi$}\!/\!\mbox{\small$\mathsf{X}$}]\rrbracket
.
By the IH, we obtain
(T_{p},\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi[\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\mathsf{max}\,\mathsf{\mathsf{X}}.\varphi$}\!/\!\mbox{\small$\mathsf{X}$}]
.
Our result,
(T_{p},\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\mathsf{max}\,\mathsf{\mathsf{X}}.\varphi
, follows by rule
vMax
.
∎

Appendix J
Lower Bounds

We provide additional examples and results related to
Section

VII
.
We start by
Example

J.1
below, which complements
Example

VII.4
by further illustrating the complexity of calculating lower bounds for formulae containing greatest fixed points.

Example J.1
.

Recall
\varphi_{4}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textbf{\tiny def}}}{{=}}\mathsf{max}\,\mathsf{\mathsf{X}}.\bigl([r][s]\mathsf{X}\wedge([c]\mathsf{ff}\vee[a]\mathsf{ff})\bigr)
from
Example

II.1
.
The proposed syntactic analysis of this formula would determine that the history lower bound for
\varphi_{4}
is 2.
Concretely, the conjunction sub-formula
[r][s]\mathsf{X}
can potentially contain an unbounded number of disjunctions due to recursion, whereas the right sub-formula contains 1 disjunction, meaning that 2 traces are required;
the lower bound across the conjunction is thus
2
.
The unfolding of
\varphi_{4}
is:

\varphi_{4}^{\prime}\;\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textbf{\tiny def}}}{{=}}\;\Bigl([r][s]\bigl(\mathsf{max}\,\mathsf{\mathsf{X}}.\bigl([r][s]\mathsf{X}\wedge([c]\mathsf{ff}\vee[a]\mathsf{ff})\bigr)\bigr)\Bigr)\wedge([c]\mathsf{ff}\vee[a]\mathsf{ff})

where the history lower bound calculation is invariant at 2.

\hfill\blacksquare

To prove
Proposition

VII.3
,
Theorem

VII.4
and
Corollary

1
from
Section

VII
, we first give a few technical developments, starting with several properties for the function
\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(-)
in
Definition

VII.1
, where the meta-function
\mathop{\textsl{fv}}(\varphi)
returns the free recursion variables in
\varphi
.

Lemma J.1
.

For all
\varphi,\psi,\chi\in\textsc{sHML}^{\vee}_{\textsc{nf}}
:

1.

\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\varphi[\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\psi$}\!/\!\mbox{\small$\mathsf{X}$}])=\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\varphi[\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\mathsf{max}\,\mathsf{\mathsf{Y}}.\psi$}\!/\!\mbox{\small$\mathsf{X}$}])

2.

\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\psi)\leq\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\chi)
implies
\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\varphi[\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\psi$}\!/\!\mbox{\small$\mathsf{X}$}])\leq\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\varphi[\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\chi$}\!/\!\mbox{\small$\mathsf{X}$}])

3.

\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\varphi)\leq\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\psi)
implies
\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\varphi)\leq\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\psi[\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\varphi$}\!/\!\mbox{\small$\mathsf{X}$}])

Proof.

We only give those for the main cases of (
3
); the others follow with a similar but more straightforward argument and can be proven independently.

The proof of (
3
)

proceeds by
induction on
\psi
.
Assume
\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\varphi)\leq\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\psi)
.
We show
\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\varphi)\leq\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\psi[\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\varphi$}\!/\!\mbox{\small$\mathsf{X}$}])
.

•

When
\psi=\mathsf{Y}
, we have
\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\mathsf{Y})\!=\!\infty
and
\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\varphi)\leq\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\mathsf{Y})
.
If
\mathsf{X}\!=\!\mathsf{Y}
, result follows immediately since

\mathsf{X}[\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\varphi$}\!/\!\mbox{\small$\mathsf{X}$}]=\varphi
.
If
\mathsf{X}\!\neq\mathsf{Y}
, result also follows immediately since
\mathsf{Y}[\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\varphi$}\!/\!\mbox{\small$\mathsf{X}$}]=\mathsf{Y}
.

•

When
\psi=[\alpha]\psi^{\prime}
, we have
\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\varphi)\leq\mathop{\textsl{lb}}([\alpha]\psi^{\prime})\!=\!\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\psi^{\prime})
.
By the IH, we deduce
\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\varphi)\leq\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\psi^{\prime}[\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\varphi$}\!/\!\mbox{\small$\mathsf{X}$}])
, which implies

\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\varphi)\leq\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(([\alpha]\psi^{\prime})[\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\varphi$}\!/\!\mbox{\small$\mathsf{X}$}])
.

•

When
\psi=\psi_{1}\wedge\psi_{2}
, we have
\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\varphi)\leq\textsl{min}(\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\psi_{1}),\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\psi_{2}))
,
which implies
\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\varphi)\leq\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\psi_{1})
and
\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\varphi)\leq\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\psi_{2})
.
By the IH, we obtain
\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\varphi)\leq\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\psi_{1}[\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\varphi$}\!/\!\mbox{\small$\mathsf{X}$}])
and
\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\psi_{2}[\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\varphi$}\!/\!\mbox{\small$\mathsf{X}$}])
.
Our result,
\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\varphi)\leq\mathop{\textsl{lb}}((\psi_{1}\wedge\psi_{2})[\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\varphi$}\!/\!\mbox{\small$\mathsf{X}$}])
, follows since
\mathop{\textsl{lb}}((\psi_{1}\wedge\psi_{2})[\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\varphi$}\!/\!\mbox{\small$\mathsf{X}$}])=\textsl{min}(\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\psi_{1}[\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\varphi$}\!/\!\mbox{\small$\mathsf{X}$}]),\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\psi_{2}[\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\varphi$}\!/\!\mbox{\small$\mathsf{X}$}]))
.

•

When
\psi=\psi_{1}\vee\psi_{2}
, then
\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\varphi)\!\leq\!\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\psi_{1}\vee\psi_{2})\!=\!\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\psi_{1})\!+\!\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\psi_{2})\!+\!1
.
There are two subcases to consider:

–

When
\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\varphi)\!\leq\!\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\psi_{1})
or
\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\varphi)\!\leq\!\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\psi_{2})
.

W.l.o.g.
suppose the former.
By the IH, we obtain
\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\varphi)\!\leq\!\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\psi_{1}[\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\varphi$}\!/\!\mbox{\small$\mathsf{X}$}])
, which implies that

\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\varphi)\!\leq\!\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\psi_{1}[\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\varphi$}\!/\!\mbox{\small$\mathsf{X}$}])+\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\psi_{2}[\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\varphi$}\!/\!\mbox{\small$\mathsf{X}$}])+1=\mathop{\textsl{lb}}((\psi_{1}\vee\psi_{2})[\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\varphi$}\!/\!\mbox{\small$\mathsf{X}$}])
.

–

When
\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\varphi)\!>\!\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\psi_{1})
and
\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\varphi)\!>\!\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\psi_{2})
.
By the IH, we obtain
\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\psi_{1})\!\leq\!\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\psi_{1}[\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\psi_{1}$}\!/\!\mbox{\small$\mathsf{X}$}])
and
\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\psi_{2})\!\leq\!\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\psi_{2}[\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\psi_{2}$}\!/\!\mbox{\small$\mathsf{X}$}])
.
But by
Lemma

J.1
(
2
), we also know
\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\psi_{1}[\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\psi_{1}$}\!/\!\mbox{\small$\mathsf{X}$}])\!\leq\!\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\psi_{1}[\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\varphi$}\!/\!\mbox{\small$\mathsf{X}$}])
and
\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\psi_{2}[\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\psi_{2}$}\!/\!\mbox{\small$\mathsf{X}$}])\!\leq\!\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\psi_{2}[\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\varphi$}\!/\!\mbox{\small$\mathsf{X}$}])
.
This implies that

\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\varphi)\leq\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\psi_{1})+\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\psi_{2})+1\!\leq\!\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\psi_{1}[\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\varphi$}\!/\!\mbox{\small$\mathsf{X}$}])+\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\psi_{2}[\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\varphi$}\!/\!\mbox{\small$\mathsf{X}$}])=\mathop{\textsl{lb}}((\psi_{1}\vee\psi_{2})[\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\varphi$}\!/\!\mbox{\small$\mathsf{X}$}])
, as required.

•

When
\psi\!=\!\mathsf{max}\,\mathsf{\mathsf{Y}}.\psi^{\prime}
, then
\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\varphi)\!\leq\!\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\mathsf{max}\,\mathsf{\mathsf{Y}}.\psi^{\prime})\!=\!\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\psi^{\prime})
.
If
\mathsf{X}\!=\!\mathsf{Y}
, our result is immediate since
(\mathsf{max}\,\mathsf{\mathsf{X}}.\psi^{\prime})[\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\varphi$}\!/\!\mbox{\small$\mathsf{X}$}]\!=\!\mathsf{max}\,\mathsf{\mathsf{X}}.\psi^{\prime}
.
If
\mathsf{X}\!\neq\!\mathsf{Y}
, then by the IH, we obtain
\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\varphi)\!\leq\!\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\psi^{\prime}[\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\varphi$}\!/\!\mbox{\small$\mathsf{X}$}])\!=\!\mathop{\textsl{lb}}((\mathsf{max}\,\mathsf{\mathsf{Y}}.\psi^{\prime})[\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\varphi$}\!/\!\mbox{\small$\mathsf{X}$}])
.
∎

Corollary 8
.

For all
\varphi\!\in\!\textsc{sHML}^{\vee}_{\textsc{nf}}
,
\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\varphi)\leq\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\varphi[\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\varphi$}\!/\!\mbox{\small$\mathsf{X}$}])
.

Proof.

Follows from
Lemma

J.1
(
3
) by letting
\varphi=\psi
.
∎

We give an alternative definition to the violation relation,
\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}
, in
Definition

VII.3
that is specific to
\textsc{sHML}^{\vee}_{\textsc{nf}}
formulae (in contrast to
\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}
which is defined over
\textsc{sHML}^{\vee}
), namely
Definition

J.1
.

Theorem

J.6
then shows that these two definitions correspond.

Definition J.1
.

The
separation violation relation
, denoted as
\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}
, is the least relation of the form
(\textsc{Hst}\times\textsc{Bool}\times\textsc{sHML}^{\vee}_{\textsc{nf}})
satisfying the following rules:

\frac{H\neq\emptyset}{(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\mathsf{ff}}\quad\textsc{{\scriptsize svF}}\frac{(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\varphi[\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\mathsf{max}\,\mathsf{\mathsf{X}}.\varphi$}\!/\!\mbox{\small$\mathsf{X}$}]}{(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\mathsf{max}\,\mathsf{\mathsf{X}}.\varphi}\quad\textsc{{\scriptsize svMax}}\frac{H^{\prime}=\mathop{\textsl{sub}}(H,\alpha)\quad\textsf{f\,}^{\prime}=\textsf{f\,}\wedge\textsc{Det}(\alpha)\quad(H^{\prime},\textsf{f\,}^{\prime})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\varphi}{(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}[\alpha]\varphi}\quad\textsc{{\scriptsize svUm}}\frac{H^{\prime}=\mathop{\textsl{sub}}(H,\gamma)\quad\textsf{f\,}^{\prime}=\textsf{f\,}\wedge\textsc{Det}(\gamma)\quad(H^{\prime},\textsf{f\,}^{\prime})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}[\alpha]\varphi}{(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}[\alpha]\varphi}\quad\textsc{{\scriptsize svUmPre}}\frac{(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\varphi}{(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\varphi\wedge\psi}\quad\textsc{{\scriptsize svAndL}}\frac{(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\psi}{(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\varphi\wedge\psi}\quad\textsc{{\scriptsize svAndR}}\frac{H=H_{1}\uplus H_{2}\quad(H_{1},\mathsf{true})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\varphi\quad(H_{2},\mathsf{true})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\psi}{(H,\mathsf{true})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\varphi\vee\psi}\quad\textsc{{\scriptsize svOr}}

We write

H\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\varphi

to mean
(H,\mathsf{true})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\varphi
.

\hfill\blacksquare

Width irrevocability also holds for the separation violation relation,
Lemma

J.2
.

Lemma J.2
.

For all
\varphi\in\textsc{sHML}^{\vee}_{\textsc{nf}}
,
if
(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\varphi
then
(H\cup H^{\prime},\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\varphi
.

Proof.

The proof proceeds by rule induction.
We only give the proof for the case when
(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\varphi
is derived via rule
svOr
; the other cases are straightforward.

•

We know
(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\varphi_{1}\vee\varphi_{2}
because
H=H_{1}\uplus H_{2}
and
(H_{1},\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\varphi_{1}
and
(H_{2},\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\varphi_{2}
.
We show that
(H\cup H^{\prime},\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\varphi_{1}\vee\varphi_{2}
.
Let
H^{\prime\prime}=H\backslash H^{\prime}
where
H_{1}\cap H^{\prime}=\emptyset
and
H_{2}\cap H^{\prime}=\emptyset
.
By the IH, we know
(H_{1}\cup H^{\prime\prime},\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\varphi_{1}
.
Since
(H_{1}\cup H^{\prime\prime})\cap H_{2}=\emptyset
, we conclude
(H\cup H^{\prime},\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\varphi_{1}\vee\varphi_{2}
via rule
svOr
.
∎

Lemma

J.4
shows that all violating systems for
\bigvee_{i\in I}[\alpha_{i}]\varphi_{i}
from
\textsc{sHML}^{\vee}_{\textsc{nf}}
can violate the sub-formulae
[\alpha_{i}]\varphi_{i}
through disjoint histories.
This result relies on the helper function
\mathop{\textsl{start}}(H,\alpha)=\{t\;|\;t=\alpha t^{\prime}\in H\}
, returning the set of all traces in
H
that are prefixed with a sequence of internal actions
\gamma\in\textsc{iAct}^{*}
, followed by an
\alpha
action.

E.g.
when
H=\{\delta_{1}rsa,\delta_{2}rsc,ars\}
, then
\mathop{\textsl{start}}(H,r)=\{\delta_{1}rsa,\delta_{2}rsc\}
.

Lemma J.3
.

For all
\alpha,\beta
such that
\alpha\neq\beta
,
\mathop{\textsl{start}}(H,\alpha)\cap\mathop{\textsl{start}}(H,\beta)=\emptyset
.

\hfill\blacksquare

Proof.

Straightforward by definition.
∎

Lemma J.4
.

For all formulae
\bigvee_{i\in I}[\alpha_{i}]\varphi_{i}\in\textsc{sHML}^{\vee}_{\textsc{nf}}
and histories
H\in\textsc{Hst}
:

\displaystyle\text{if }(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\bigvee_{i\in I}[\alpha_{i}]\varphi_{i}

\displaystyle\text{ then }\bigl(\mathop{\textsl{start}}(H,\alpha_{i}),\textsf{f\,}\bigr)\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}[\alpha_{i}]\varphi_{i}

\displaystyle\qquad\text{ for each }i\in I

Proof.

The proof proceeds by induction on the size of
I
.

•

For the base case,
I=\{1\}
,
i.e.,

(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}[\alpha]\varphi
.
Using
Lemma

I.4
, we know
\exists t\in\textsc{iAct}^{*}
such that
H^{\prime}=\mathop{\textsl{sub}}(H,t\alpha)
and
\textsf{f\,}^{\prime}=\textsf{f\,}\wedge\textsc{Det}(t\alpha)
and
(H^{\prime},\textsf{f\,}^{\prime})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi
.
Let
H^{\prime\prime}=\{\alpha u\;|\;u\in H^{\prime}\}
,
i.e.,
all traces in
H^{\prime}
prefixed with action
\alpha
.
Applying rule
svUm
, we obtain
(H^{\prime\prime},\textsf{f\,}\wedge\textsc{Det}(t))\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}[\alpha]\varphi
.
Let
H^{\prime\prime\prime}=\{tt^{\prime}\;|\;t^{\prime}\in H^{\prime}\}
,
i.e.,
all traces in
H^{\prime\prime}
prefixed with trace
t
.
Applying rule
svUmPre

n
times where
n
is the length of trace
t
, we obtain
(H^{\prime\prime},\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}[\alpha]\varphi
.
Since, by definition,
H^{\prime\prime\prime}\subseteq\mathop{\textsl{start}}(H,\alpha)
, we can use
Lemma

J.2
to conclude that
(\mathop{\textsl{start}}(H,\alpha),\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}[\alpha]\varphi
.

•

For the inductive case,
I{=}\{1,...,n{+}1\}
.
The judgment
(H,\textsf{f\,})\!\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\!\bigvee_{i{\in}I}[\alpha_{i}]\varphi_{i}
can be expanded to

(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\bigl([\alpha_{1}]\varphi_{1}\bigr)\vee\bigl(\bigvee_{j\in J}[\alpha_{j}]\varphi_{j}\bigr)\text{ where }J=\{2,\ldots,n{+}1\}

By case analysis, this could have only been derived via rule
svOr
, which means that
there exist
H^{\prime},H^{\prime\prime}
such that
H=H^{\prime}\uplus H^{\prime\prime}
and

(H^{\prime},\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}[\alpha_{1}]\varphi_{1}
and
(H^{\prime\prime},\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\bigvee_{j\in J}[\alpha_{j}]\varphi_{j}
.
Using
Lemma

J.2
, we deduce

(p,H)\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}[\alpha_{1}]\varphi_{1}
and
(p,H)\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\bigvee_{j\in J}[\alpha_{j}]\varphi_{j}
.
By the IH, we obtain
(\mathop{\textsl{start}}(H,\alpha_{1}),\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}[\alpha_{1}]\varphi_{1}
and
(\mathop{\textsl{start}}(H,\alpha_{j}),\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}[\alpha_{j}]\varphi_{j}
for all
j\in J
.
We can thus conclude
(\mathop{\textsl{start}}(H,\alpha_{i}),\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}[\alpha_{i}]\varphi_{i}
for all
i\in I
, as required.
∎

We show
a similar result holds for the violation relation
\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}
.

Lemma J.5
.

For all
\bigvee_{i\in I}[\alpha_{i}]\varphi_{i}\in\textsc{sHML}^{\vee}_{\textsc{nf}}
and
H\subseteq T_{p}
:

\displaystyle\text{if }(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\bigvee_{i\in I}[\alpha_{i}]\varphi_{i}

\displaystyle\text{ then }\bigl(\mathop{\textsl{start}}(H,\alpha_{i}),\textsf{f\,}\bigr)\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}[\alpha_{i}]\varphi_{i}

\displaystyle\qquad\text{ for each }i\in I

Proof.

Proof is similar, but more straightforward, to that for
Lemma

J.5
.
∎

Theorem J.6

(Correspondence)
.

For all
\varphi{\in}\textsc{sHML}^{\vee}_{\textsc{nf}}
and
H{\in}\textsc{Hst}
,
(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi
iff
(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\varphi
.

Proof.

For the
if
direction, we show
(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\varphi
implies
(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi
.
The proof is by rule induction.
We only give the case for when
(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi
is derived via rule
svOr
; all other cases are homogeneous.

•

We know
(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\varphi\vee\psi
because
\exists H_{1},H_{2}
such that
H=H_{1}\uplus H_{2}
,

(H_{1},\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\varphi
and
(H_{2},\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\psi
.
By the IH, we deduce
(H_{1},\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi
and
(H_{2},\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\psi
,
which imply
(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi
and
(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\psi
by
Proposition

I.2
.
Our result,
(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi\vee\psi
, follows via rule
vOr
.

For the
only if
direction, we show
(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi
implies
(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\varphi
.
Again, the proof is by rule induction and we only give that for when
(p,H)\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi
is derived via rule
vOr
.

•

We know
(H,\textsf{f\,})\!\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\!\varphi\vee\psi
because
(H,\textsf{f\,})\!\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\!\varphi
and
(H,\textsf{f\,})\!\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\!\psi
.
By the IH, we deduce
(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\varphi
and
(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\psi
.
Since
\varphi\vee\psi\!\in\textsc{sHML}^{\vee}_{\textsc{nf}}
, then
\varphi\!=\!\bigvee_{i\in I}[\alpha_{i}]\varphi_{i}
and
\psi\!=\!\bigvee_{j\in J}[\alpha_{j}]\psi_{j}
where
I\cap J=\emptyset
.
By
Lemma

J.4
, we obtain

\bigl(\mathop{\textsl{start}}(H,\alpha_{i}),\textsf{f\,}\bigr)\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\varphi_{i}
and

\bigl(\mathop{\textsl{start}}(H,\alpha_{j}),\textsf{f\,}\bigr)\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\psi_{j}
for each
i\in I
and
j\in J
.
By
Lemma

J.3
, we also know
\bigcap_{k\in I\cup J}\mathop{\textsl{start}}(H,\alpha_{k})=\emptyset
.
Repeatedly applying rule
svOr
, we deduce

\bigl(\bigcup_{i\in I}\mathop{\textsl{start}}(H,\alpha_{i}),\textsf{f\,}\bigr)\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\bigvee_{i\in I}[\alpha_{i}]\varphi_{i}
and

\bigl(\bigcup_{j\in J}\mathop{\textsl{start}}(H,\alpha_{j}),\textsf{f\,}\bigr)\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\bigvee_{j\in J}[\alpha_{i}]\psi_{j}
.
By rule
svOr
again, we get
\bigl(\bigcup_{k\in I\cup J}\mathop{\textsl{start}}(H,\alpha_{k}),\textsf{f\,}\bigr)\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\varphi\vee\psi
.
Our result,
(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\varphi\vee\psi
, follows via
Lemma

J.2
since
\bigcup_{k\in I\cup J}\mathop{\textsl{start}}(H,\alpha_{k})\subseteq H
.
∎

Equipped with these technical results, we prove
Proposition

VII.3
and
Theorem

VII.4
.

Theorem

VII.4
is a direct consequence of
Lemma

J.7
.

See
VII.3

Proof.

Assume
(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi\vee\psi
.
Since
\varphi\vee\psi\in\textsc{sHML}^{\vee}_{\textsc{nf}}
, we know
\varphi=\bigvee_{i\in I}[\alpha_{i}]\varphi_{i}^{\prime}
and
\psi=\bigvee_{j\in J}[\alpha_{j}]\psi_{j}^{\prime}
where
I\cap J=\emptyset
.
By
Lemma

J.5
, we deduce
(\mathop{\textsl{start}}(H,\alpha_{i}),\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}[\alpha_{i}]\varphi_{i}^{\prime}
and
(\mathop{\textsl{start}}(H,\alpha_{j}),\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}[\alpha_{j}]\psi_{j}^{\prime}
for each
i\in I
and
j\in J
.
Let
H_{1}=\bigcup_{i\in I}\mathop{\textsl{start}}(H,\alpha_{i})
and
H_{2}=\bigcup_{j\in J}\mathop{\textsl{start}}(H,\alpha_{j})
.
Repeatedly applying rule
vOr
, we obtain
(H_{1},\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi
and
(H_{2},\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\psi
.
From
Lemma

J.3
, we know
H_{1}\cap H_{2}=\emptyset
.
Let
H_{3}=H\backslash H_{1}
.
By
Proposition

I.2
, we get
(H_{1},\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\varphi
and
(H_{2}\cup H_{3},\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}\psi
where
H=H_{1}\uplus(H_{2}\cup H_{3})
as required.
∎

Lemma J.7
.

For all

\varphi\!\in\!\textsc{sHML}^{\vee}_{\textsc{nf}}
and
H\!\in\!\textsc{Hst}
, if
(H,\textsf{f\,})\!\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\!\varphi
then

|H|\geq\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\varphi)\!+\!1
.

Proof.

The proof is by rule induction.

•

Case
vF
,
i.e.,

(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\mathsf{ff}
where
H\neq\emptyset
. Thus
|H|\geq 1=\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\mathsf{ff})+1
.

•

Case
vUm
,
i.e.,

(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}[\alpha]\varphi
because
(H^{\prime},\textsf{f\,}^{\prime})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\varphi
where
H^{\prime}=\mathop{\textsl{sub}}(H,\alpha)
and
\textsf{f\,}^{\prime}=\textsf{f\,}\wedge\textsc{Det}(\alpha)
.
By the IH, we deduce
|H^{\prime}|\geq\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\varphi)\!+\!1
.
Let
H^{\prime\prime}=\{\alpha t\;|\;t\in H^{\prime}\}
.
Since
H^{\prime\prime}\subseteq H
, then
|H|\geq|H^{\prime\prime}|=|H^{\prime}|\geq\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\varphi)+1=\mathop{\textsl{lb}}([\alpha]\varphi)+1
.

•

Case
vUmPre
,
i.e.,

(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}[\alpha]\varphi
because
(H^{\prime},\textsf{f\,}^{\prime})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}[\alpha]\varphi
where
H^{\prime}=\mathop{\textsl{sub}}(H,\gamma)
and
\textsf{f\,}^{\prime}=\textsf{f\,}\wedge\textsc{Det}(\gamma)
.
By the IH, we deduce
|H^{\prime}|\geq\mathop{\textsl{lb}}([\alpha]\varphi)+1
.
Let
H^{\prime\prime}=\{\gamma t\;|\;t\in H^{\prime}\}
.
Since
H^{\prime\prime}\subseteq H
, then
|H|\geq|H^{\prime\prime}|=|H^{\prime}|\geq\mathop{\textsl{lb}}([\alpha]\varphi)+1
.

•

Case
vAndL
,
i.e.,

(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\varphi\wedge\psi
because
(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\varphi
.
By the IH,
|H|\geq\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\varphi)+1\geq\textsf{min}(\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\varphi),\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\psi))+1=\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\varphi\wedge\psi)+1
.

•

Case
vAndR
. Analogous to previous case.

•

Case
vOr
,
i.e.,

(H,\mathsf{true})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\varphi\vee\psi
because
H=H_{1}\uplus H_{2}
and
(H_{1},\mathsf{true})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\varphi
and
(H_{2},\mathsf{true})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\varphi
.
By the IH, we obtain
|H_{1}|\geq\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\varphi)+1
and
|H_{2}|\geq\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\psi)+1
, which means that

|H|=|H_{1}|+|H_{2}|\geq\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\varphi)+\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\psi)+2=\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\varphi\vee\psi)+1
.

•

Case
vMax
,
i.e.,

(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\mathsf{max}\,\mathsf{\mathsf{X}}.\varphi
because
(H,\textsf{f\,})\models_{\scriptscriptstyle\textsc{Det}}^{s}\varphi[\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\mathsf{max}\,\mathsf{\mathsf{X}}.\varphi$}\!/\!\mbox{\small$\mathsf{X}$}]
.
By the IH,
Lemma

J.1
(
1
) and
Corollary

8
, we conclude

|H|\geq\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\varphi[\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\mathsf{max}\,\mathsf{\mathsf{X}}.\varphi$}\!/\!\mbox{\small$\mathsf{X}$}])+1=\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\varphi[\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\varphi$}\!/\!\mbox{\small$\mathsf{X}$}])+1\geq\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\varphi)+1=\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\mathsf{max}\,\mathsf{\mathsf{X}}.\varphi)+1
, as required.
∎

See
VII.4

Proof.

Follows from
Lemma

J.7
and
Theorem

J.6
.
∎

See
1

Proof.

Suppose
\varphi\in\textsc{sHML}^{\vee}_{\textsc{nf}}
and
H\in\textsc{Hst}
such that
\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\varphi)=\infty
.
Since histories are finite,
|H|\leq\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\varphi)<\mathop{\textsl{lb}}(\varphi)+1=\infty
.
Our result,
H\not{\models_{Det}}\varphi
, follows by the contrapositive of
Theorem

VII.4
.
∎
